# Work Ledger

Append-only. Every agent adds a line when it starts and when it finishes a chunk, so work survives
token exhaustion. Format: `- YYYY-MM-DD [agent] STATUS: what (files / commit)`.
STATUS ∈ START, DONE, BLOCKED, HANDOFF. **Next session: read "Now / Next" first.**

- 2026-11-07 [backend-dev] DONE: root cause (reproduced with the real backup image, read-only root, tmpfs /tmp): NOT a missing writable path (HOME=/tmp in the image; forget --prune succeeds). `forget --prune` needs restic's exclusive lock; any other lock (a running export, or one left by a killed Job, which only counts as stale after 30 min and from another host) makes it fail, and MODE=backup never runs `restic unlock` (only prune/forget/purge do) and the script discards stderr. Platform retention only ever runs inside the backup Job, so nothing self-heals. Fix (Python only, no image re-pin): a backup that fails with "retention cleanup failed" queues a MODE=prune follow-up (unlocks stale locks, then same policy): platform via new trigger `prune` on platform_backup_runs (excluded from backups list/_due, row kept 1 day, audit platform_backup.pruned/prune_failed, run.error gets a "clean-up run was queued" hint); per-app db/bucket via the existing `prune` trigger in app/backups.py. New tests/test_backup_retention_lock.py (7) + focused suites 201 passed. Docs: audit actions platform_backup.pruned/prune_failed. Follow-up for Chas-approved re-pin: add `unlock` before retention in MODE=backup and stop discarding forget's stderr. NEEDS-SECURITY-REVIEW: no (no Job security context/volume change).

- 2026-11-06 [manager] DONE: Chas's real platform backup download failed with "the snapshot is not a MySQL dump". Cause: the export Job had no /work scratch and the root filesystem is read-only, so gzip couldn't write; the script's message misleads. Fake-runner tests couldn't see it. Reproduced in the real backup image (amd64, read-only root): no /work gives that exact error, with /work it reaches the upload step. Fixed with scratch_mib=256 on exports (app/platform_backup.py), test asserts the rendered Job mounts /work. No image change. .venv rebuilt on Python 3.13; CLAUDE.md says python3.13. NOT yet re-tested end to end in production.

- 2026-11-02 [backend-dev] DONE: Kaisen hang watchdog NEEDS-SECURITY-REVIEW (new POST /kaisen/builds/{id}/runs/{rid}/interrupt, worker liveness change); frontend NOT browser-checked (typecheck+lint+build+check:layout clean). Migration a9c4e1f7b2d6 (single head; kaisen_runs += last_progress_at, hang_retries, attempt, error_reason; up/down/up tested on SQLite). New app/kaisen/watchdog.py: progress stamp on turn start, model response, each tool, deployer steps, reviewer turns; sweep() = stale running runs (no stamp for KAISEN_HANG_SECONDS) -> CAS on (id, attempt) bumps `attempt`, hang_retries+1, status queued, closed inbox kind=hang note 'No progress for N min; interrupted, retrying (k/N).'; past KAISEN_HANG_RETRIES -> status error, error_reason 'hung', 'Hung N times; Resume to retry.'. Fencing: run_step carries its `attempt`; _still_active/stop_run/review check it, and a locking `_fenced` read before the turn commit discards a late tool/model result of an abandoned step. Model calls go through builder.bounded_create (hard wall; ModelTimeout is a hang, not an error; Anthropic client idle timeout now KAISEN_MODEL_CALL_SECONDS, was 600). Worker: tick(threaded=True) in prod runs the step on a daemon thread watched by worker.guarded_step (abandons on attempt change from elsewhere or a tool over KAISEN_TOOL_SECONDS); heartbeat thread (DB-free, in-memory stamps only) touches the liveness file only while watchdog.loop_healthy() (stamp < HANG+MARGIN); the main loop calls sweep() via Worker.sweep_if_due; Worker.recover() on start; reconciler (API pod) calls sweep_session as backstop; Lease got a mutex. kaisen-worker.yaml probe (120 s stale, 30 s x3) left unchanged: a wedged loop stops touching ~660 s after its last progress, pod restarts about 3 min later. Interrupt endpoint: any project member (same access as Run), 404 outsider/other-build run, 409 when not running, audit kaisen.interrupt, not counted as a hang retry. Run JSON gains hang_retries, error_reason, notes[]; BuildProgress.vue shows 'Interrupt and retry' + notes. Env (all validated, app/config.py): KAISEN_MODEL_CALL_SECONDS=300, KAISEN_MODEL_CALL_MAX_SECONDS=540, KAISEN_TOOL_SECONDS=420, KAISEN_HANG_SECONDS=600 (must exceed the two bounds, import fails otherwise), KAISEN_HANG_RETRIES=2; constants KAISEN_HANG_MARGIN_SECONDS=120, KAISEN_WATCHDOG_POLL_SECONDS=5. Docs for tech-writer: ADMIN_GUIDE Kaisen bullet (no settings table there), KAISEN_OPS section 2 (liveness now conditional, hang path, interrupt route), API route list, user guide (Interrupt and retry). Known limits: an abandoned model call may still bill unmetered tokens; a hung tool thread is abandoned, not killed (daemon, fenced). Tests: tests/test_kaisen_watchdog.py 26 passed; adjusted test_kaisen_qa_phase4.py head assertion to the new revision. Full suite 2577 passed, 16 skipped (before 3 extra tests; watchdog.py 26 own tests); ruff clean; no new crash reports after the heartbeat thread was made DB-free (hang sweep runs in the main loop via Worker.sweep_if_due, reconciler backstop).

Root cause: backup NetworkPolicy excludes private ranges, but inside the DO VPC Spaces resolves to a private IP (reconciler.py _private_storage_ips; tenants get allow-egress-storage), so restic could not connect and silently retried. Fix: platform-managed allow-egress-spaces policy (+ Role rights in backup.yaml, needs deploy), step progress lines, timeouts, backoffLimit 0, poll backstop deadline+120s. Needs image republish + deploy.yml before rerun.

- GAP (Spaces per-bucket keys): DO API `POST /v2/spaces/keys` DOES support per-bucket `grants` (read/readwrite),
  but PUT/PATCH can only rename a key; grants are immutable. So read-only toggle and rotation = create new key,
  delete old, rename. The app's access key therefore CHANGES on quota flip/rotation: `ObjectStore.set_read_only`
  now returns new creds (or None), quota.py persists them and sets app.status='pending'. NEXT (backend-dev/reconciler):
  make sure a re-render with changed Secret actually rolls the pod (e.g. checksum annotation); wire `poll_quotas`
  into the scheduler with `MySQLAdminClient` + `SpacesStore.from_env()`; wire `get_object_store`/
  `get_mysql_admin_factory` in routers/apps.py are the injection points.

- 2026-10-18 [backend-dev] DONE: `app/models.py` — `DbServer` (name unique, host/port, admin creds
  Fernet-encrypted via `app/crypto`, `max_databases` default 25 per manager's call — DO's 1 GiB managed
  MySQL caps at 75 connections, ÷5 per app user ≈ 25 lightly-used apps with headroom — status

  checked with upgrade → downgrade → upgrade round-trip against a throwaway sqlite file). `app/db_placement.py`
  — `choose_server(session)`: among `status='active'` `DbServer` rows, picks the one with the most free
  slots (`max_databases` − its `AppDatabase` count), ties broken by lowest id; raises `NoDbCapacity` if
  none qualify. Pure SQLAlchemy, no provider calls. `app/providers/mysql.py`: real `MySQLAdminClient`,
  constructed per `DbServer` (host/port/admin creds come from `__init__`, not per call — updated
  `MySQLAdmin`'s docstring in `base.py` to say so, and gave `FakeMySQLAdmin` an optional unused `server`
  param for the same calling convention without breaking its existing no-arg test). `create_db_user`
  creates `app_<id>` db + user with a random 32-char password and `MAX_USER_CONNECTIONS 5`, grants ALL
  scoped to that one db; `drop` does `DROP DATABASE/USER IF EXISTS`; `db_size_bytes` sums
  `information_schema.TABLES.(data_length+index_length)`; `set_read_only` revokes/restores only
  INSERT/UPDATE/CREATE/ALTER (SELECT/DELETE/DROP always stay granted so a quota-exceeding app can still
  read and clean up). Identifiers are our own `app_<id>` generation but still regex-validated before
  SQL interpolation (MySQL has no parameterized identifiers); passwords go through query parameters
  (`IDENTIFIED BY %s`) since PyMySQL escapes those client-side. Tests: `tests/test_db_placement.py`
  (7 cases, plain SQLite, no providers) and `tests/test_mysql_admin.py` (`@pytest.mark.mysql`, module-level
  skip unless `MYSQL_ADMIN_URL` is set — create+grant, cross-app isolation via `USE`, size reporting,
  read-only blocks INSERT but allows SELECT/DELETE, drop). Registered the `mysql` marker in new
  `pytest.ini`. CI: added a `mysql-integration` job to `.github/workflows/ci.yml` with a `mysql:8.4`
  service (root password `test`, healthcheck via `mysqladmin ping`), `MYSQL_ADMIN_URL=mysql://root:test@127.0.0.1:3306`,

- 2026-10-18 [backend-dev] DONE: `app/reconciler.py` — `reconcile_app(session, app, *, kube,
  mysql_admin_factory, object_store, settings)`: provisions an `AppDatabase` via
  `db_placement.choose_server` + `mysql_admin_factory(server).create_db_user` (NoDbCapacity ->
  status='error'/status_detail='no database capacity') and an `AppStorage` via
  `object_store.create_bucket_and_key`, both skipped if already provisioned (idempotent); builds
  `db.*`/`storage.*` refs (decrypting stored secrets via `app/crypto`), decrypts env vars, resolves
  `${{ }}` refs via `app/env_refs.resolve`, renders manifests via `app/k8s_manifests`, and
  `kube.apply()`s them — status goes 'deploying' then whatever `kube.get_status()` returns (e.g.
  'running'). Any provider exception anywhere in the flow sets status='error' with a fixed,
  hand-written `status_detail` string (never `str(exc)`) so a provider's exception text — which could
  echo a password/connection string — can never leak into status_detail; verified in
  `test_provider_failure_sets_error_without_leaking_secrets`. `delete_app(...)`: best-effort
  `kube.delete_namespace` + mysql `drop` + storage `delete` + row cleanup for soft-deleted apps,
  independent try/excepts so one failure doesn't block the others. `reconcile_all(...)`: sweeps
  pending/deploying apps through `reconcile_app` and deleted-but-not-status='deleted' apps through
  `delete_app`. Model changes: `App.status_detail` (nullable String(255)) and a new minimal
  `AppStorage` model (one-to-one with App, mirrors `AppDatabase`'s pattern — `secret_key_encrypted`
  Fernet via `app/crypto`, bucket/endpoint/region/access_key plain, size_bytes/over_quota for later
  quota polling) + relationship on `App`. Migration `c3a9f5d2b1e4` (adds `apps.status_detail`,
  creates `app_storage`) — upgrade/downgrade/upgrade round-trip verified against a throwaway sqlite
  file. `tests/test_reconciler.py` (7 cases, fakes only, plain SQLite session — no HTTP layer):
  happy path db+storage (asserts the rendered k8s Secret's `DATABASE_URL` is the real resolved
  `mysql://...` URL, no `${{ }}` leftover), placement onto the least-loaded `DbServer`, no capacity ->
  error, idempotency (second `reconcile_app` call creates no second `AppDatabase`/`AppStorage` row
  and calls the fake provider's create exactly once), provider failure -> error status with a
  secret-free detail, deletion cleanup (namespace/db/storage all gone), `reconcile_all` sweep over a
  mix of a pending and a deleted app. `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/ -q` -> **93 passed, 1
  skipped** (the skip is the pre-existing `test_mysql_admin.py` module, no `MYSQL_ADMIN_URL` here).
  Not committed (scm-security's job). Out of scope for this chunk, per the hard time budget: no
  APScheduler wiring (left as a TODO in the module docstring, per the chunk spec), no real

Every quota flip / kill result logs at WARNING. DB: `set_read_only(True)` now returns {listed,killed,failed,list_error}; re-enforced (sessions killed again) on every poll while over; if kills can't be confirmed (failed>0, listing failed, or 0 sessions visible) the app's pod is rolled once on the flip as a fallback. New proc picopaas_ops.kill_app_sessions in deploy/mysql/statefulset.yaml (only applies on a fresh in-cluster data volume; SQL not run against a live server). UNVERIFIED: whether DO managed `doadmin` can KILL other users' threads or even SEE them in PROCESSLIST (no PROCESS => silently empty list); the code tolerates both and logs; QUESTIONS.md asks Chas to check the first prod over-cap log line. Storage key ordering: a swapped key's predecessor is revoked only once the app is out of `pending` (rollout done), so reads never break; cost = old read-write key (writes) survives rollout + <=1 storage poll (<=5 min), accepted since storage overshoot is cheap and a read outage is not. New: POST /apps/{id}/storage/delete, GET /apps/{id}/storage/usage, GET /apps/{id}/database/usage (app/routers/usage.py), ObjectStore.usage/delete_objects, AppOut.quota, UI Usage section + FreeStorageDialog + 'over cap' badge, CLI status usage + storage-delete, docs, .github/workflows/quota-e2e.yml (weekly; skips itself without E2E secrets), extended tests/e2e quota tests. (2) GET /admin/projects?q&limit&offset&include_personal -> {items,total,limit,offset}, no params = old plain list. (3) 502 root cause: the UI's generic "Request failed (HTTP 502)" means the body had no JSON detail, i.e. NOT our deliberate GitHubError->502 (that always has a detail); it is a proxy-generated 502/504 because the push ran synchronously (1 GitHub call per file, 30s timeout each) in the request. Admin audit is cookie-only so prod evidence could not be read. Fix: POST /kaisen/builds/{id}/push is now async (202 + kaisen_pushes job, GET .../push/{push_id}, detail.push), GitHubError has code/status with actionable messages. Root cause: tests/test_kaisen_uploads_convo.py::test_migration_up_and_down runs alembic in-process, and alembic/env.py's fileConfig() disabled every existing logger, so later caplog tests saw ''.
